HoloAegis: Frozen Representation, Topological Inference --- Minimally Parametric Safety Manifolds and Their Capability Boundaries for LLM Guardrails
Authors: Tak Ho Alex Li, Kaijie Liu, Lik-Hang Lee, Kin Chung Ho, Ping Shum, Michael K. Ng
First: 2026-08-09T05:05:12+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-15T12:22:48+00:00
Comments: Preprint v2, September 2026. 4 figures, 12 tables. Corrected and substantially revised from v1 (arXiv:2608.08485v1)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety guardrails face a fundamental tension: fine-tuning distorts pre-trained representations while generative judges incur prohibitive inference costs. We ask a complementary question: how far can safety be achieved through pure geometric reasoning over frozen representations, and where does it fail? We present HoloAegis, a minimally parametric topological inference framework that decouples representation from reasoning: an un-fine-tuned encoder maps text to the unit sphere S^{d-1}, and all decisions reduce to Gibbs-Boltzmann free-energy differences over pre-computed anchor centroids. We contribute a boundary-mapping study rather than a leaderboard claim. On a frozen three-benchmark protocol, HoloAegis (3.2 MB) statistically matches WildGuard-7B (14 GB) on toxicity (0.96 vs. 0.96), exceeds it on harmful behaviors (0.99 vs. 0.79), and cedes oversafety detection (0.62 vs. 0.98) -- while ShieldGemma-2B fails on indirect harms (0.34). These failure modes are complementary and mechanistically traceable: potential-difference scoring senses manifold clustering, whereas policy-conditioned LLM judging requires explicit taxonomy matching. We restate our Topological Boundary Stability conjecture in ratio form and validate it via reference-set bootstrap: anchor banks reduce score variance 4-15x and boundary displacement to approximately 0.44 + 0.23 sqrt(k/K) of the full-space estimator. Per-domain analysis further reveals that geometric separability tracks within-domain semantic homogeneity. Our results chart where geometric guardrails substitute for, and where they must defer to, LLM judges.
中文标题/摘要
标题：HoloAegis：冻结表示、拓扑推理 --- 最小参数安全流形及其对LLM保护措施的能力边界
当前LLM安全保护措施面临根本性矛盾：微调扭曲了预训练表示，而生成性判断则产生了高昂的推理成本。我们提出一个互补性问题：通过对冻结表示的纯几何推理，安全性可以达到多远，在哪里会失败？我们提出HoloAegis，一个最小参数的拓扑推理框架，将表示与推理解耦：未微调的编码器将文本映射到单位球面S^{d-1}，所有决策归结为对预计算锚心的Gibbs-Boltzmann自由能差异。我们贡献了一个边界映射研究，而不是排行榜声明。在一个冻结的三基准协议上，HoloAegis（3.2 MB）在毒性上与WildGuard-7B（14 GB）统计匹配（0.96 vs. 0.96），在有害行为上超越它（0.99 vs. 0.79），而在过度安全检测上则让步（0.62 vs. 0.98）——同时ShieldGemma-2B在间接危害上失败（0.34）。这些失败模式是互补的，并且在机制上可追溯：电位差评分感知流形聚类，而政策条件的LLM判断则需要明确的分类匹配。我们以比率形式重述我们的拓扑边界稳定性猜想，并通过参考集自助法进行验证：锚库将得分方差降低4-15倍，边界位移约为全空间估计器的0.44 + 0.23 sqrt(k/K)。每个领域的分析进一步揭示几何可分性与领域内语义同质性之间的关系。我们的结果描绘了几何保护措施替代和必须依赖LLM判断的地方。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of current large language model (LLM) safety guardrails, which struggle with the trade-off between fine-tuning that alters pre-trained representations and the high costs associated with generative judges. Previous methods often rely on fine-tuning or complex inference processes, leading to inefficiencies and potential inaccuracies. The proposed HoloAegis framework introduces a minimally parametric approach that utilizes frozen representations for topological inference, allowing for geometric reasoning without distorting the underlying data. This method demonstrates significant contributions by achieving comparable performance to larger models on toxicity detection while surpassing them in identifying harmful behaviors, thus validating the effectiveness of geometric reasoning in safety measures. The methodology involves mapping text to a unit sphere and making decisions based on Gibbs-Boltzmann free-energy differences, which has shown to reduce score variance and improve boundary stability across various domains.
本研究解决了当前LLM安全保护措施的局限性，这些措施在微调预训练表示和生成判断器的高成本之间存在权衡。现有方法往往会扭曲表示或产生高昂的推理成本，因此作者提出了HoloAegis，这是一种最小参数的拓扑推理框架，利用冻结表示进行几何推理。该方法允许决策仅基于预计算锚心的Gibbs-Boltzmann自由能差异，从而保持原始表示的完整性。论文贡献了一项边界映射研究，展示了HoloAegis在冻结三基准协议上的表现，其毒性检测与WildGuard-7B相当，同时在有害行为检测上超越了它。研究结果表明，几何保护措施在某些情况下可以有效替代LLM判断器，同时也识别出LLM仍然必要的领域。
Useless but Safe? Benchmarking Utility Recovery with User Intent Clarification in Multi-Turn Conversations
Authors: Mingqian Zheng, Malia Morgan, Liwei Jiang, Carolyn Rose, Maarten Sap
First: 2026-04-29T18:37:18+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-12T19:48:42+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety alignment techniques improve model robustness against adversarial attacks, but overlook whether and how LLMs can recover helpfulness when benign users clarify their intent. We introduce CarryOnBench, the first interactive benchmark that measures whether LLMs can revise their interpretation of user intent and recover utility, while remaining safe through multi-turn conversations. Starting from 398 seemingly harmful queries with benign underlying intents, we simulate 5,970 conversations by varying user follow-up sequences, evaluating 14 models on both intent-aligned utility and safety. CarryOnBench yields 1,866 different conversation flows of 4--12 turns, totaling 23,880 model responses. We design Ben-Util, a checklist-based metric that evaluates how well each model response fulfills the user's benign information need using atomic items. At turn one, models fulfill only 10.5--37.6% of the user's benign information need. When the same query includes the benign intent upfront, models fulfill 25.1--72.1%, confirming that models withhold information due to intent misinterpretation, not limited knowledge. With benign clarifications in multi-turn conversations, 13 of 14 models approach or exceed this single-turn baseline, yet recovery cost varies across models. We identify two failure modes invisible to single-turn evaluations---unsafe recovery, where a model updates at disproportionate safety cost and redundant recovery, where a model recycles prior responses rather than providing new information. Moreover, conversations converge to similar harmfulness levels regardless of how conservative the model starts. These findings expose a gap that single-turn evaluations miss---whether a model is appropriately cautious or simply unresponsive to clarified user intent.
中文标题/摘要
标题：无用但安全？在多轮对话中通过用户意图澄清基准测试实用性恢复
当前的LLM安全对齐技术提高了模型对对抗攻击的鲁棒性，但忽视了LLM在良性用户澄清意图时是否以及如何恢复有用性。我们引入了CarryOnBench，这是第一个交互式基准，测量LLM是否能够修正对用户意图的解释并恢复实用性，同时在多轮对话中保持安全。从398个看似有害的查询开始，这些查询具有良性的潜在意图，我们通过变化用户后续序列模拟了5,970个对话，评估了14个模型在意图对齐的实用性和安全性方面的表现。CarryOnBench产生了1,866种不同的对话流程，轮次为4到12，总计23,880个模型响应。我们设计了Ben-Util，这是一个基于清单的指标，评估每个模型响应在多大程度上满足用户的良性信息需求。在第一轮中，模型仅满足用户良性信息需求的10.5%到37.6%。当同一查询在前面包含良性意图时，模型满足25.1%到72.1%，确认模型因意图误解而隐瞒信息，而不是知识有限。在多轮对话中，13个模型接近或超过这一单轮基线，但恢复成本在模型之间有所不同。我们识别出两种在单轮评估中不可见的失败模式——不安全恢复，其中模型以不成比例的安全成本进行更新，以及冗余恢复，其中模型重复先前的响应而不是提供新信息。此外，无论模型起始多么保守，对话都趋向于相似的有害性水平。这些发现揭示了单轮评估所忽视的一个差距——模型是否适当地谨慎，或仅仅对澄清的用户意图无反应。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of current large language model (LLM) safety alignment techniques, which enhance robustness against adversarial attacks but fail to assess the models' ability to recover helpfulness when users clarify their intent in multi-turn conversations. Previous methods primarily focused on single-turn evaluations, which overlook the dynamics of user intent clarification and often misinterpret benign queries as harmful. The proposed approach, CarryOnBench, introduces an interactive benchmark that evaluates LLMs on their ability to revise interpretations and recover utility while maintaining safety across multi-turn dialogues. The methodology involves simulating 5,970 conversations based on 398 seemingly harmful queries, assessing 14 models for intent-aligned utility and safety using a new checklist-based metric, Ben-Util. The findings reveal that models initially fulfill only a fraction of benign information needs but improve significantly with clarified intent, highlighting the importance of multi-turn evaluations in understanding model performance and exposing failure modes that single-turn assessments miss.
本研究解决了当前大型语言模型（LLM）安全对齐技术的局限性，这些技术专注于抵御对抗性攻击，但未能评估模型在用户澄清意图时在多轮对话中恢复有用性的能力。以往的方法主要在单轮上下文中评估模型，导致对模型在多次交互中如何解释用户意图的理解不足。所提出的方法CarryOnBench引入了一个交互式基准，模拟了5970个对话，以评估14个模型在保持安全的同时与用户意图对齐的能力。研究表明，在第一轮中，模型仅满足10.5%至37.6%的良性信息需求，但在澄清后，14个模型中有13个提高了性能，尽管恢复成本各异。研究结果揭示了单轮评估忽视的两种失败模式：不安全的恢复和冗余的恢复，表明多轮评估对于更好地理解模型在现实应用中的行为是必要的。
Adaptive Adversaries: A Multi-Turn, Multi-LLM Benchmark for LLM Agent Security
Authors: Devina Jain, David Hartmann, Chuan Li
First: 2026-07-20T15:30:38+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-11T18:06:53+00:00
Comments: Second Workshop on Agents in the Wild: Safety, Security, and Beyond
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
LLM-based agents process external content, exposing them to prompt injection and multi-turn manipulation. We present a 21-scenario benchmark for adaptive cross-session attacks against fresh-session LLM defenders: an autonomous LLM attacker observes prior defender responses and pivots across rounds, while each defender response is evaluated as a fresh interaction. A controlled 3 x 3 attacker-defender matrix contains 945 battles. Restricting scoring to the first round yields 0-1% attack success rate (ASR); allowing 15 rounds yields 7.9-16.8%. Pooling three attacker LLMs uncovers 1.7-2.2 times as many unique successful inputs as the best single attacker, at three times the battle budget. Aggregate rates conceal opposing scenario-specific weaknesses in session-secret protection and authority handling, preserved in two higher-sample evaluations. On six scenarios, adding one provenance paragraph reduces ASR from 110/270 to 70/270, with selective effects across tasks. History and defender-state controls, together with frozen replay, characterize how the interaction protocol changes the result. A competition adds 18,422 held-out battles on a fixed gpt-oss-20b backbone and complementary benign-task evaluations. The benchmark exposes attacker and defender models, harnesses, scenarios, session state, and interaction budgets as configurable choices for systematic security evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自适应对手：多回合、多LLM基准测试用于LLM代理安全
基于LLM的代理处理外部内容，使其暴露于提示注入和多回合操控中。我们提出了一个21场景基准，用于针对新会话LLM防御者的自适应跨会话攻击：自主LLM攻击者观察先前防御者的响应并在回合间进行调整，而每个防御者的响应被评估为一次新的互动。一个受控的3 x 3攻击者-防御者矩阵包含945场战斗。将评分限制在第一回合的攻击成功率（ASR）为0-1%；允许15回合则为7.9-16.8%。汇聚三个攻击者LLM发现的独特成功输入数量是最佳单一攻击者的1.7-2.2倍，战斗预算是三倍。汇总率掩盖了会话秘密保护和权限处理中的相对场景特定弱点，这在两个更高样本评估中得以保留。在六个场景中，添加一个来源段落将ASR从110/270降低到70/270，任务间效果选择性。历史和防御者状态控制，以及冻结重放，表征了互动协议如何改变结果。一个竞赛在固定的gpt-oss-20b基础上增加了18,422场保留战斗和补充的良性任务评估。该基准揭示了攻击者和防御者模型、工具、场景、会话状态和互动预算作为系统安全评估的可配置选择。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the vulnerabilities of LLM-based agents to prompt injection and multi-turn manipulation, which have been inadequately assessed by previous methods that often overlook adaptive attack strategies. The proposed approach introduces a comprehensive 21-scenario benchmark for evaluating adaptive cross-session attacks, where an autonomous LLM attacker learns from prior defender responses and adapts its strategy over multiple rounds. This method effectively highlights the limitations of existing defenses, demonstrating that allowing for more interaction rounds significantly increases the attack success rate from 0-1% to 7.9-16.8%. The paper contributes by providing a structured framework for evaluating LLM agent security through a controlled attacker-defender matrix, revealing specific weaknesses in session-secret protection and authority handling, and offering insights into how interaction protocols influence outcomes. The methodology includes a competition with 18,422 held-out battles, enhancing the robustness of the evaluation and supporting the goal of improving LLM security assessments.
本研究关注基于LLM的代理在提示注入和多轮操控方面的脆弱性，而这些在以往研究中评估不足。现有方法往往未能考虑利用先前交互的自适应攻击，导致对LLM安全性的理解有限。本文提出了一种新基准，包括21种场景，用于评估自适应跨会话攻击，其中自主LLM攻击者根据先前的防御者响应调整策略。该方法涉及一个受控的3 x 3攻击者-防御者矩阵，共有945场战斗，结果显示，允许多轮交互显著提高了攻击成功率，从0-1%上升到7.9-16.8%。研究结果表明，汇集多个攻击者LLM可以产生更多独特的成功输入，突显了会话秘密保护和权威处理方面的特定弱点，从而为LLM安全性提供了更全面的评估。
'Ghaib in Translation' aka Unseen Harm: Measuring Cross-Script Safety Inconsistency with 'Missed-in-Urdu' Scores in LLM Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Fawzia Zehra, Kara-Isitt, Sonal Khosla, Stephen Swift
First: 2026-08-25T07:58:40+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-09T16:54:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Urdu, the world's tenth most spoken language with 246 million speakers, remains almost entirely absent from mainstream LLM safety evaluation and nine years of WOAH proceedings. To investigate whether this absence has measurable consequences for content moderation reliability, five large language models, GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4.5, Gemini 2.5 Flash, Qwen-2.5, and Llama-3.1, were tested across six datasets spanning Nastaliq Urdu, Roman Urdu, English, and code-switched Urdu-English. Across the five Urdu-script datasets, label instability between original-script and English-translation classification ranged from 15.9% (Gemini 2.5 Flash) to 31.6% (Qwen-2.5), with a 'Missed-in-Urdu' rate, content flagged as harmful in English translation but passed as normal in the original script, ranging from 2.4% to 9.9% (median 4.3%). A complete enumeration of all 205 papers across nine ALW/WOAH editions via the ACL Anthology API confirms zero dedicated Urdu papers across the entire period. Results indicate that current LLMs provide uneven safety assurance across Urdu's script varieties, with smaller open-weight models showing substantially higher instability and missed-harm rates than frontier closed models.
中文标题/摘要
标题：'翻译中的隐秘'即未见的危害：使用'乌尔都语遗漏'分数测量跨脚本安全不一致性在LLM仇恨言论检测中的应用
乌尔都语是全球第十大语言，拥有2.46亿说话者，但在主流LLM安全评估和九年的WOAH会议中几乎完全缺席。为了调查这种缺席是否对内容审核的可靠性产生可测量的影响，测试了五个大型语言模型：GPT-4o、Claude Sonnet 4.5、Gemini 2.5 Flash、Qwen-2.5和Llama-3.1，涵盖了六个数据集，包括Nastaliq乌尔都语、罗马乌尔都语、英语和代码切换的乌尔都语-英语。在五个乌尔都语脚本数据集中，原始脚本与英语翻译分类之间的标签不稳定性范围为15.9%（Gemini 2.5 Flash）到31.6%（Qwen-2.5），'乌尔都语遗漏'率，即在英语翻译中标记为有害但在原始脚本中通过的内容，范围为2.4%到9.9%（中位数4.3%）。通过ACL Anthology API对九个ALW/WOAH版本中205篇论文的完整枚举确认，在整个期间内没有专门的乌尔都语论文。结果表明，当前的LLM在乌尔都语的脚本变体之间提供的不均匀安全保障，较小的开放权重模型显示出明显更高的不稳定性和遗漏危害率，超过了前沿的封闭模型。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the significant gap in safety evaluation for Urdu, a widely spoken language that has been largely overlooked in large language model (LLM) assessments. Previous methods failed to adequately evaluate the safety of content in different scripts, leading to inconsistencies in harm detection. The proposed approach systematically tests five LLMs across multiple datasets, revealing a concerning range of label instability and missed harm rates between original Urdu scripts and their English translations. The study contributes to the understanding of cross-script safety inconsistency in LLMs and highlights the urgent need for dedicated evaluation frameworks for Urdu. The methodology involves analyzing the performance of LLMs on Nastaliq Urdu, Roman Urdu, and code-switched datasets, demonstrating that current models exhibit significant variability in safety assurance, with smaller models showing higher missed harm rates, thus supporting the need for improved content moderation strategies.
本研究关注乌尔都语这一广泛使用语言在大型语言模型（LLMs）安全评估中的显著缺口，这一问题在主流评估和以往的会议中被忽视。过去的方法未能充分评估乌尔都语内容审核的可靠性，导致不同书写体之间的安全措施不一致。所提出的方法强调了“乌尔都语遗漏现象”，即有害内容在乌尔都语和英语翻译之间被错误分类，揭示了现有评估中的关键疏漏。本研究通过定量测量各种数据集和LLMs之间的安全不一致性作出了贡献，表明当前模型在乌尔都语书写体数据集中的表现存在显著差异。该方法论涉及在多个数据集上测试五个LLMs，揭示标签不稳定率高达31.6%，遗漏有害内容的比例高达9.9%，表明这些模型的表现未能满足乌尔都语内容审核所需的安全标准。
Style Over Substance: Content-Invariant Wrappers Flip LLM Safety-Judge Verdicts
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Wenbo Ye, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Junwei Yao
First: 2026-09-08T04:27:22+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-08T04:27:22+00:00
Comments: 8 pages, 1 table. Code, wrappers, and per-verdict labels: https://github.com/Yongxi-Zhou/safety-judge-robustness
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Automatic safety judges -- systems such as Llama Guard or a GPT-4o grading prompt that decide whether a model's reply is harmful -- produce the numbers behind almost every reported jailbreak success rate, defense evaluation, and safety leaderboard. We ask whether these judges grade what a reply contains or how it sounds. We keep a reply's content fixed and add content-invariant style wrappers: fixed strings placed before or after the reply that change only its tone (an educational disclaimer, a fake safety "reasoning" block, a token refusal followed by the unchanged harmful body), or, on harmless refusals, framing that merely sounds dangerous. The body is preserved byte-for-byte, so a faithful judge must return the same verdict, and any flip is an error of the judge, not a change in safety. Over 600 JailbreakBench replies x up to 7 forms x 8 judges, we measure flip rates with paired significance tests and measured noise floors. Findings are precise rather than universal: most judges barely move, but specific judges harbor cheaply exploitable blind spots. A token-refusal wrapper flips 19.9% of GPT-4o-mini's correct "unsafe" verdicts (noise floor 0.5%; 18.2% under majority-of-three re-scoring) yet moves Claude only 0.4%. The deployed Llama Guard 4 is deterministically gamed: an "educational course" framing flips 12.3% of its harmful verdicts to safe. A second deployed guard (gpt-oss-safeguard-20b) is immune, and rewriting only the grading prompt (StrongREJECT-style) cuts the attack tenfold on the identical model -- the vulnerability lives in the judge, not the content. A two-annotator human validation confirms 100% content invariance and 90% of flips as judge errors (kappa 0.95-1.0), and a bootstrap shows the underlying model ranking is already unstable to sampling alone. We release the dataset, wrappers, code, and per-verdict labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：形式胜于内容：内容不变的包装翻转LLM安全评判结果
自动安全评判系统——如Llama Guard或GPT-4o评分提示，决定模型回复是否有害——生成几乎所有报告的越狱成功率、防御评估和安全排行榜背后的数据。我们探讨这些评判是根据回复的内容还是其表述方式进行评分。我们保持回复内容不变，添加内容不变的风格包装：在回复前或后放置的固定字符串，仅改变其语气（教育免责声明、虚假的安全“推理”块、后接不变的有害内容的拒绝令），或者在无害拒绝的情况下，仅仅听起来危险的框架。主体逐字保留，因此一个忠实的评判必须返回相同的判决，任何翻转都是评判的错误，而不是安全性的变化。在超过600个JailbreakBench回复中，乘以最多7种形式和8个评判，我们通过配对显著性测试和测量噪声底线来测量翻转率。发现是精确的而非普遍的：大多数评判几乎没有变化，但特定评判存在廉价可利用的盲点。一个令牌拒绝包装翻转了19.9%的GPT-4o-mini的正确“危险”判决（噪声底线0.5%；在三人多数重新评分下为18.2%），但对Claude的影响仅为0.4%。已部署的Llama Guard 4被确定性地操控：“教育课程”框架将12.3%的有害判决翻转为安全。第二个已部署的防护（gpt-oss-safeguard-20b）是免疫的，仅重写评分提示（StrongREJECT风格）就将攻击减少十倍，脆弱性存在于评判中，而非内容。两位注释员的人类验证确认100%的内容不变性和90%的翻转为评判错误（kappa 0.95-1.0），而自助法显示基础模型排名已经对抽样不稳定。我们发布数据集、包装、代码和每个判决标签。
Summary / 总结
This research investigates the reliability of automatic safety judges, which evaluate whether responses from language models are harmful, by questioning if their judgments are based on content or merely stylistic presentation. Previous methods have focused on content evaluation but have shown vulnerabilities to manipulation through stylistic changes, leading to inconsistent safety verdicts. The proposed approach introduces content-invariant style wrappers that alter the tone of responses without changing their content, effectively testing the judges' robustness. The study employs a comprehensive methodology involving over 600 responses and various judges to measure the flip rates of safety judgments. The findings reveal that while most judges remain stable, certain judges exhibit exploitable weaknesses, with specific wrappers significantly altering verdicts, indicating that the flaws lie within the judges rather than the content itself, thus contributing to a deeper understanding of safety evaluation mechanisms in language models.
本研究探讨了自动安全评估系统的可靠性，这些系统评估模型回复的有害性，并质疑它们是评估内容还是仅仅评估呈现风格。以往的方法主要集中在内容评估上，但往往忽视了风格变化如何影响判断，从而导致安全评估的潜在脆弱性。提出的方法引入了内容不变的风格包装，这些包装在不改变内容的情况下改变回复的语气，有效测试评估者的一致性。该研究采用了综合方法，涉及超过600个回复和多种风格修改，以测量多个评估者的判断翻转率。研究结果表明，尽管大多数评估者保持一致的评估，但某些评估者表现出可利用的弱点，特定的包装导致判决发生显著变化，从而突显了安全评估系统需要改进鲁棒性的必要性。
The Oversight Gap: What LLM Safety Monitors Miss, and Why It Is Not Capability
Authors: Xin Xu
First: 2026-09-07T07:57:28+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-07T07:57:28+00:00
Comments: 11 pages, 3 figures. Under review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Several properties safety monitors are asked to certify, among them cross-tenant noninterference, sandbagging and evaluation awareness, are 2-safety hyperproperties, witnessed only by two executions. The standard consequence is a binary impossibility: one trace cannot decide them. We replace the binary with a measurement. A tight bound puts the balanced accuracy of any single-trace monitor at $\tfrac12+\tfrac12\,TV(P_0,P_1)$, turning undecidability into a graded detectability frontier and defining an oversight gap: a monitor's shortfall below it. On a leak family with closed-form $TV$, nine LLM monitors are optimal at $TV=0$ but capture little signal as $TV$ grows; at $TV=1$, where a 20-line membership check scores $100\%$, they average $60.9\%$. That shortfall is mostly not capability: naming what to check closes $61\%$ of it while leaving the $TV=0$ control at chance. The same split runs through a $2{\times}2$ factorial: an imagined second run leaves monitors at chance ($50.4\%$) while the same rule on an executed second run reaches $90.0\%$, and a stored oracle without a comparison procedure yields only $68.2\%$. Information and procedure are each necessary and neither is capability. Under nondeterminism, replay tracks a closed-form $k$-replay curve only under the right projection, and a projection frontier shows the resulting dilemma is forced: narrow misses $98.6\%$ of off-channel leaks, broad flags $75.7\%$ of clean traffic, and attainable accuracy decays like $1/(qm)$ in the benign-variation rate and the channel count. Finally, two frontier LLM judges certified an earlier version of our own benchmark as sound while a sign test found a directional bias ($p=2.7\times10^{-5}$) that invalidated three of our findings. Construction validity for hyperproperty benchmarks should be proved mechanically, not audited by models.
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing safety monitors for large language models (LLMs), which struggle to certify complex properties like cross-tenant noninterference and evaluation awareness due to their binary impossibility nature. Traditional methods fail to provide a nuanced understanding of these properties, leading to significant oversight gaps in detection capabilities. This paper proposes a new measurement-based approach that replaces binary assessments with a graded detectability framework, allowing for a more precise evaluation of monitor performance. The methodology involves analyzing the balanced accuracy of various LLM monitors against a defined oversight gap, revealing that while some monitors perform optimally at low total variation (TV), their effectiveness diminishes as TV increases. The findings indicate that a significant portion of the performance shortfall is not due to inherent capability limitations but rather the lack of specific checks, with the proposed method achieving up to 90% accuracy in certain conditions, thus supporting the need for improved monitoring frameworks in LLM safety assessments.
本文探讨了当前大型语言模型（LLMs）安全监控器的局限性，特别是它们无法认证某些仅通过两次执行可观察的2-安全超属性。以往的方法依赖于二元评估，导致在这些属性的决策中出现根本的不可能性。所提出的方法引入了一种分级可检测性度量，定义了一个监督差距，量化监控器在理论阈值以下的不足。研究方法涉及分析九个LLM监控器在不同总变差（TV）指标下的表现，结果表明，尽管某些监控器在TV=0时表现最佳，但随着TV的增加，其有效性下降。研究发现，短缺的很大一部分并非由于能力，而是由于所使用的信息和程序，所提方法在TV=1时的平均准确率为60.9%，因此突显了对超属性评估改进基准和验证过程的需求。
SAEs Can Improve Unlearning: Dynamic Sparse Autoencoder Guardrails for Precision Unlearning in LLMs
Authors: Aashiq Muhamed, Jacopo Bonato, Mona Diab, Virginia Smith
First: 2025-04-11T01:24:03+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-07T03:32:59+00:00
Comments: COLM 2025
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Machine unlearning is a promising approach to improve LLM safety by removing unwanted knowledge from the model. However, prevailing gradient-based unlearning methods suffer from issues such as high computational costs, hyperparameter instability, poor sequential unlearning capability, vulnerability to relearning attacks, low data efficiency, and lack of interpretability. While Sparse Autoencoders are well-suited to improve these aspects by enabling targeted activation-based unlearning, prior approaches underperform gradient-based methods. This work demonstrates that, contrary to these earlier findings, SAEs can significantly improve unlearning when employed dynamically. We introduce $\textbf{Dynamic DAE Guardrails}$ (DSG), a novel method for precision unlearning that leverages principled feature selection and a dynamic classifier. Our experiments show DSG substantially outperforms leading unlearning methods, achieving superior forget-utility trade-offs. DSG addresses key drawbacks of gradient-based approaches for unlearning -- offering enhanced computational efficiency and stability, robust performance in sequential unlearning, stronger resistance to relearning attacks, better data efficiency including zero-shot settings, and more interpretable unlearning.
中文标题/摘要
标题：SAEs可以改善遗忘：用于LLMs精确遗忘的动态稀疏自编码器护栏
机器遗忘是一种有前景的方法，通过从模型中移除不需要的知识来提高LLM的安全性。然而，现有的基于梯度的遗忘方法存在高计算成本、超参数不稳定、顺序遗忘能力差、易受重学习攻击、数据效率低和缺乏可解释性等问题。稀疏自编码器非常适合通过启用针对性激活的遗忘来改善这些方面，但先前的方法在性能上不如基于梯度的方法。本研究表明，与早期发现相反，SAEs在动态使用时可以显著改善遗忘。我们引入了$\textbf{动态DAE护栏}$（DSG），这是一种利用原则性特征选择和动态分类器的精确遗忘新方法。我们的实验表明，DSG在遗忘方法中显著优于领先的遗忘方法，实现了更好的遗忘-效用权衡。DSG解决了基于梯度的遗忘方法的关键缺陷——提供了更高的计算效率和稳定性、在顺序遗忘中的强大性能、对重学习攻击的更强抵抗力、更好的数据效率（包括零样本设置）以及更可解释的遗忘。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenges of machine unlearning in large language models (LLMs), particularly the limitations of existing gradient-based methods which are hindered by high computational costs, instability, and vulnerability to relearning attacks. Previous methods have struggled with efficiency and interpretability, prompting the exploration of Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) as a potential solution. This paper proposes a novel approach called Dynamic DAE Guardrails (DSG), which utilizes dynamic feature selection and a classifier to enhance unlearning precision. The methodology demonstrates that DSG significantly outperforms traditional unlearning techniques, achieving better forget-utility trade-offs and addressing the shortcomings of gradient-based methods, thus supporting the goal of improving LLM safety through effective unlearning.
本文探讨了机器遗忘中的挑战，特别是在大型语言模型（LLMs）中，现有的基于梯度的方法面临高计算成本和较差可解释性等问题。以往的方法在超参数不稳定性和对重新学习攻击的脆弱性方面表现不佳，因此需要更有效的解决方案。所提出的方法动态稀疏自编码器保护（DSG）利用稀疏自编码器通过启用针对性激活的遗忘能力来增强遗忘能力，已被证明优于传统方法。研究方法涉及利用原则性特征选择和动态分类器来实现精确遗忘。实验结果表明，DSG显著改善了遗忘-效用权衡，并解决了基于梯度的方法的局限性，展示了在遗忘任务中提高的效率、稳定性和可解释性。
Behind Harmful Compliance: Behavioral and Mechanistic Divergence Across LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Md Rysul Kabir, Zoran Tiganj
First: 2026-04-20T17:01:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-05T18:10:56+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Open-weight language models can be rendered unsafe through several parameter-level interventions, yet models with matched harmful compliance can exhibit fundamentally different failure modes. We compare harmful supervised fine-tuning (SFT), harmful reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), and refusal-feature abliteration in Qwen2.5-7B and Llama-3.1-8B using harmfulness, capability, self-audit, safety reflection, representation similarity, and refusal-direction repair. All three routes reach near-ceiling harmfulness, but SFT causes the broadest capability loss and representational drift; abliteration yields localized, family-dependent refusal-feature suppression; and RLVR largely preserves base-model capability, explicit safety judgments, and representation geometry while retargeting behavior toward compliance. RLVR models consequently remain unusually responsive to safety-reflection prompts despite complying under direct prompting. We further find that harmful RLVR induces capability-blind compliance: models claim to complete unavailable actions and fabricate information about nonexistent entities. Targeted RLVR calibration substantially reduces this false acceptance without restoring safety or degrading general capability. These results show that harmful compliance, harm recognition, and capability awareness are separable behavioral axes, and that self-audit and hallucination patterns are not necessarily robust safety signals under adaptive post-training.
中文标题/摘要
标题：有害合规背后的行为和机制差异：LLM越狱案例分析
开放权重语言模型可以通过多种参数级干预变得不安全，但具有匹配有害合规的模型可能表现出根本不同的失败模式。我们比较了有害的监督微调（SFT）、有害的可验证奖励强化学习（RLVR）和Qwen2.5-7B与Llama-3.1-8B中的拒绝特征消融，使用有害性、能力、自我审计、安全反思、表示相似性和拒绝方向修复。所有三种途径都达到了接近上限的有害性，但SFT导致了最广泛的能力损失和表示漂移；消融产生了局部的、依赖于家族的拒绝特征抑制；而RLVR在重新定向行为以实现合规的同时，基本上保留了基础模型的能力、明确的安全判断和表示几何。因此，尽管在直接提示下合规，RLVR模型仍对安全反思提示表现出异常的响应性。我们进一步发现，有害的RLVR会导致能力盲目的合规：模型声称完成不可用的动作，并虚构关于不存在实体的信息。针对性的RLVR校准显著减少了这种错误接受，而不恢复安全或降低一般能力。这些结果表明，有害合规、伤害识别和能力意识是可分离的行为轴，自我审计和幻觉模式在适应性后训练下不一定是稳健的安全信号。
Summary / 总结
This research investigates the safety vulnerabilities of open-weight language models, particularly focusing on how different parameter-level interventions can lead to harmful compliance with varying failure modes. Previous methods, including harmful supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and harmful reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), have shown limitations such as significant capability loss and representational drift. The proposed approach, RLVR, addresses these issues by preserving base-model capabilities while retargeting behavior towards compliance, thus maintaining responsiveness to safety-reflection prompts. The paper contributes to the understanding of harmful compliance and capability awareness as distinct behavioral axes, employing a comparative analysis of SFT, RLVR, and refusal-feature abliteration on models like Qwen2.5-7B and Llama-3.1-8B. The findings reveal that while all methods achieve high levels of harmfulness, RLVR notably reduces false acceptance of unavailable actions without compromising overall model capability, supporting the goal of enhancing safety in language models.
本研究探讨了开放权重语言模型的安全漏洞，特别是不同参数级干预如何导致具有不同失败模式的有害合规性。以往的方法，包括有害的监督微调（SFT）和有害的可验证奖励强化学习（RLVR），显示出显著的能力损失和表征漂移等局限性。所提出的方法RLVR通过保持基础模型能力和增强安全反思，同时重新定向行为以实现合规，解决了这些问题。该研究有助于理解有害合规机制的差异，并表明有害的RLVR可能导致能力盲目合规，即模型生成关于不存在实体的虚假信息。该方法比较了SFT、RLVR和拒绝特征消融对Qwen2.5-7B和Llama-3.1-8B模型的影响，达到了接近顶点的有害性，同时揭示了有害合规性与能力意识的可分性，从而支持了对语言模型改进安全措施的需求。
TIER: Threat Implicitness Benchmark for Evaluating LLM Safety Behaviors
Authors: Thu-Hien Trinh-Thi, Hai-Yen Vong, Thanh-Ha Ung-Dung, Tram Ho
First: 2026-09-04T13:13:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-04T13:13:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Current LLM safety benchmarks largely rely on binary metrics, overlooking how models respond to harmful prompts with varying threat implicitness. We introduce TIER, a Threat Implicitness Benchmark for behavioral safety evaluation of LLMs. TIER covers four risk domains and four threat levels, from explicit harmful requests to sophisticated jailbreaks. Responses are assessed using a six-label behavior scale and two independent LLM judges. Experiments on six open-weight LLMs show that safety behaviors evolve gradually across threat levels rather than shifting directly from refusal to compliance. Contextual prompts yield the most diverse behaviors, while jailbreaks reveal the largest robustness gaps. Furthermore, models with similar Attack Success Rates can exhibit distinct response distributions, highlighting the need for behavior-aware LLM safety evaluation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TIER：评估LLM安全行为的威胁隐含性基准
当前LLM安全基准主要依赖于二元指标，忽视了模型对不同威胁隐含性的有害提示的响应。我们引入了TIER，一个用于LLM行为安全评估的威胁隐含性基准。TIER涵盖四个风险领域和四个威胁级别，从明确的有害请求到复杂的越狱。使用六级行为量表和两个独立的LLM评审对响应进行评估。在六个开放权重的LLM上进行的实验表明，安全行为在威胁级别之间逐渐演变，而不是直接从拒绝转变为遵从。上下文提示产生了最丰富的行为，而越狱则揭示了最大的鲁棒性差距。此外，具有相似攻击成功率的模型可能表现出不同的响应分布，突显了对行为感知的LLM安全评估的需求。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of current LLM safety benchmarks, which primarily use binary metrics and fail to account for the nuances of model responses to harmful prompts with varying levels of threat implicitness. Previous methods have not adequately captured the complexity of these interactions, leading to an oversimplified understanding of LLM safety. The proposed TIER benchmark introduces a more nuanced evaluation framework that categorizes threats into four risk domains and levels, allowing for a detailed assessment of model behaviors using a six-label scale and independent judges. This methodology reveals that LLM safety behaviors evolve gradually across threat levels, with contextual prompts eliciting diverse responses and jailbreaks exposing significant robustness gaps. The findings underscore the necessity for a behavior-aware approach to LLM safety evaluation, demonstrating that models with similar attack success rates can still display markedly different response patterns, thus contributing valuable insights into the safety dynamics of LLMs.
本研究解决了当前LLM安全基准的局限性，这些基准主要使用二元指标，未能捕捉模型对具有不同威胁隐含性的有害提示的细微响应。以往的方法未能充分评估安全行为的广度，导致对模型脆弱性的理解不完整。提出的TIER基准引入了一个更全面的评估框架，将威胁分为四个风险领域和层次，利用六个标签的行为量表由独立评审进行评估。这种方法揭示了LLM的安全行为在威胁层次上逐渐演变，情境提示产生了最丰富的响应，而越狱攻击则暴露了显著的鲁棒性差距。研究结果强调了对LLM安全评估采取行为意识方法的必要性，表明具有相似攻击成功率的模型仍可能产生显著不同的响应模式，从而为对LLM安全行为的更细致理解做出了贡献。
IndicSafeEval: Safety Robustness of Large Language Models under Multilingual Persuasive Jailbreak Attacks
Authors: Saikat Mondal, Mamta, Deeksha Varshney, Oana Cocarascu, Asif Ekbal
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-09-03T12:54:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-04T11:46:25+00:00
Comments: 38 pages, 7 figures, 33 tables. Accepted to Findings of EMNLP 2026. Contains examples of harmful model outputs
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in multilingual settings, yet their safety is still evaluated primarily in English. This limits our understanding of how alignment failures manifest in low-resource and culturally diverse languages. We introduce IndicSafeEval, a persuasion-based jailbreak evaluation framework for Indian languages. Our benchmark combines ten safety critical content categories with six human-like persuasive strategies across four different Indian languages, such as Hindi, Bengali, Marathi and Punjabi, resulting in 7,200 adversarial prompts. We conduct a systematic black-box evaluation of several open-source LLMs to examine how their safety behaviour varies across languages, persuasion strategies, and risk categories. Our analysis shows that the model does not behave equally safely across all languages and prompt styles. Instead, safety performance depends strongly on both the languages used and the way a request is phrased using persuasive cues. We further observe that different risk categories exhibit different levels of vulnerability, with some types of harmful content being significantly more susceptible to persuasion-based jailbreaks than others. These findings reveal important limitations of current safety evaluations, which are largely English-centric, and underscore the need for multilingual and persuasion-aware benchmarking frameworks to more accurately assess real-world LLM safety. Our implementation is available at https://github.com/MonSaikat/IndicSafeEval. Warning: this paper contains example data that may be offensive or harmful.
中文标题/摘要
标题：IndicSafeEval：多语言说服性越狱攻击下大型语言模型的安全鲁棒性

FLY-EVAL++: An Evidence-Driven Evaluation Protocol for Safety-Constrained Flight Prediction with Large Language Models
Authors: Yalun Wu, Junfeng Fang, Jiawei Wang, Haotian Liu, Qijun Yang, Minghan Yang, Hongcheng Guo, Zhoujun Li, Boyang Wang
Venue: Proceedings of the Conference on Language Modeling (COLM), 2026
First: 2026-09-03T16:00:02+00:00 · Latest: 2026-09-03T16:00:02+00:00
Comments: Published as a conference paper at COLM 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Evaluating large language models (LLMs) in safety-critical, physics-governed environments requires more than accuracy-based metrics, because predictions that are numerically close to the ground truth can still violate operational constraints, combine fields in physically inconsistent ways, or fail to produce usable structured outputs. Existing evaluation protocols do not measure these failure modes reliably. We propose FLY-EVAL++, an evidence-driven evaluation protocol that combines deterministic verification of protocol compliance, physical feasibility, and safety constraints with fixed rubric-guided aggregation into interpretable multi-dimensional scores. We instantiate FLY-EVAL++ for Flight Trajectory and Attitude Prediction (FTAP) by extending the PilotBench setting with history-conditioned and multi-step prediction tasks. Across 66 LLMs, safety compliance is the most discriminative dimension of model behavior: models with comparable predictive performance differ by more than 28 points in safety score, and we observe recurrent failures including safety violations under physically plausible predictions and instability in multi-step rollouts. These results show that evaluation in safety-critical domains should measure constraint satisfaction and structured validity explicitly rather than rely on accuracy-centric reporting alone.
中文标题/摘要
标题：FLY-EVAL++：一种基于证据的安全约束飞行预测评估协议
在安全关键、物理约束的环境中评估大型语言模型（LLMs）需要的不仅仅是基于准确性的指标，因为数值上接近真实值的预测仍然可能违反操作约束、以物理不一致的方式组合领域，或未能生成可用的结构化输出。现有的评估协议无法可靠地测量这些失败模式。我们提出了FLY-EVAL++，一种基于证据的评估协议，结合了对协议合规性、物理可行性和安全约束的确定性验证，以及通过固定评分标准引导的聚合，形成可解释的多维评分。我们通过扩展PilotBench设置，结合历史条件和多步预测任务，为飞行轨迹和姿态预测（FTAP）实例化FLY-EVAL++。在66个LLM中，安全合规性是模型行为中最具区分性的维度：具有可比预测性能的模型在安全评分上相差超过28分，我们观察到反复出现的失败，包括在物理上合理的预测下的安全违规和多步展开中的不稳定性。这些结果表明，在安全关键领域的评估应明确测量约束满足和结构有效性，而不是仅依赖于以准确性为中心的报告。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the need for improved evaluation methods for large language models (LLMs) in safety-critical environments, where traditional accuracy-based metrics fall short by not capturing operational constraints and physical inconsistencies. Existing evaluation protocols lack reliability in measuring these failure modes, prompting the authors to propose FLY-EVAL++, an evidence-driven evaluation protocol that integrates deterministic verification of compliance with safety constraints and physical feasibility, along with a structured scoring system. The contribution of this paper lies in its novel approach to evaluating flight trajectory and attitude prediction tasks, demonstrating that safety compliance is a critical dimension of model performance, with significant discrepancies observed among models despite similar predictive accuracy. The methodology involves applying FLY-EVAL++ across 66 LLMs, revealing recurrent failures in safety violations and instability during multi-step predictions, thus highlighting the necessity for evaluations that prioritize constraint satisfaction and structured validity over mere accuracy.

Validity-Aware Jailbreak Evaluation for Large Language Models
Authors: Qilong Wu, Sahil Wadhwa, Pranab Mohanty, Giri Iyengar, Varun Chandrasekaran
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-31T23:57:53+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T23:57:53+00:00
Comments: To appear on EMNLP 2026 main
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Jailbreak robustness has become central to large language model (LLM) safety evaluation, yet prevailing methodologies rely primarily on refusal behavior, semantic resemblance, and intent-matching heuristics that emphasize linguistic plausibility rather than correctness. We identify a key limitation in existing evaluations: many jailbreak intents depend on instructional validity rather than epistemic factuality, allowing realistic-looking responses to be labeled successful despite being factually or procedurally incorrect. To address this gap, we propose Sequential Epistemic and Action-Level Validation (SEAV), a verification-centric jailbreak evaluation framework that decomposes responses into ordered steps and evaluates both validity and correctness. SEAV combines LLM-as-a-judge mechanisms for semantic interpretation with retrieval-grounded verification using external knowledge sources, assessing whether generated content is factually correct, structurally consistent, and operationally capable of advancing harmful objectives. Empirically, SEAV cuts the false-positive rate on SD-A (a curated strategic-dishonesty diagnostic) by 14.9\,pp vs. the strongest baseline, and reclassifies 22.1\%--51.0\% of sampled prior-labeled successes as invalid across three of four public benchmarks. Together, these results show that enforcing correctness substantially reshapes measured robustness: many previously labeled jailbreak successes are reclassified as invalid, and results are stable across the tested search backends and evaluator models. Code and data are available at https://github.com/Ardor-Wu/SEAV.
中文标题/摘要
标题：针对大型语言模型的有效性感知越狱评估
越狱的鲁棒性已成为大型语言模型（LLM）安全评估的核心，但现有的方法主要依赖于拒绝行为、语义相似性和意图匹配启发式，这些方法强调语言的合理性而非正确性。我们识别出现有评估的一个关键限制：许多越狱意图依赖于指令有效性而非认知事实性，允许看似真实的响应被标记为成功，尽管在事实或程序上是错误的。为了解决这一问题，我们提出了顺序认知和行动级验证（SEAV），这是一个以验证为中心的越狱评估框架，它将响应分解为有序步骤，并评估有效性和正确性。SEAV结合了LLM作为评判机制的语义解释与使用外部知识源的检索基础验证，评估生成内容是否在事实上正确、结构上连贯，并在操作上能够推进有害目标。实证结果表明，SEAV将SD-A（一个策划的战略不诚实诊断）的假阳性率降低了14.9个百分点，相较于最强基线，并在四个公共基准中的三个重新将22.1%至51.0%的先前标记的成功重新分类为无效。这些结果表明，强制正确性显著重塑了测量的鲁棒性：许多先前标记的越狱成功被重新分类为无效，且结果在测试的搜索后端和评估模型中是稳定的。代码和数据可在https://github.com/Ardor-Wu/SEAV获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical issue of jailbreak robustness in large language models (LLMs), highlighting limitations in existing evaluation methods that focus on linguistic plausibility rather than factual correctness. Previous approaches primarily relied on refusal behavior and intent-matching heuristics, which often misclassify responses as successful despite factual inaccuracies. The proposed Sequential Epistemic and Action-Level Validation (SEAV) framework improves upon these methods by decomposing responses into ordered steps and assessing both validity and correctness through a combination of LLM-as-a-judge mechanisms and retrieval-grounded verification. This methodology significantly reduces the false-positive rate on the SD-A diagnostic by 14.9 percentage points compared to the strongest baseline and reclassifies a substantial portion of previously labeled successes as invalid. These findings demonstrate that prioritizing correctness can fundamentally alter the assessment of jailbreak robustness, with stable results across various benchmarks and models supporting the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLM）中的越狱鲁棒性问题，指出现有评估方法的局限性，这些方法主要关注语言的合理性而非事实的正确性。以往的方法主要依赖拒绝行为和意图匹配启发式，常常错误地将响应标记为成功，尽管它们在事实上的不准确。提出的顺序认知和行动级验证（SEAV）框架通过将响应分解为有序步骤并评估有效性和正确性，提供了一种新颖的方法，结合了LLM作为评判机制与基于检索的验证。该方法显著提高了评估准确性，在SD-A诊断中将假阳性率降低了14.9个百分点，并在多个基准测试中重新分类了大量先前标记为成功的案例为无效。这些发现表明，优先考虑正确性可以从根本上改变对LLM中越狱鲁棒性的评估。
Risk-Adjusted Harm Scoring for Automated Red Teaming for LLMs in Financial Services
Authors: Fabrizio Dimino, Bhaskarjit Sarmah, Stefano Pasquali
First: 2026-03-11T14:14:13+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T14:24:11+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Existing LLM safety evaluations rely on binary attack-success rates and domain-agnostic taxonomies, leaving regulated Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) deployments exposed to failures elicited through legally or professionally plausible framing. We introduce RAHS (Risk-Adjusted Harm Score), a risk-sensitive metric jointly capturing disclosure severity, disclaimer mitigation, and inter-judge agreement, and FinRedTeamBench, a 989-prompt benchmark spanning seven BFSI risk areas and 34 sub-categories mapped to regulatory frameworks. Evaluation uses an ensemble of three heterogeneous LLM judges, validated against human experts, and an adaptive multi-turn red-teaming pipeline. On nine open-weight models, RAHS preserves separation under near-ceiling ASR, ranking is stable under hyperparameter sweeps, and multi-turn pressure drives not only more jailbreaks but more operationally severe disclosures, exposing failure modes that single-turn, domain-agnostic evaluations cannot reveal.
中文标题/摘要
标题：金融服务中针对大型语言模型的自动红队风险调整危害评分
现有的大型语言模型安全评估依赖于二元攻击成功率和领域无关的分类法，使受监管的银行、金融服务和保险（BFSI）部署面临通过合法或专业上合理的框架引发的失败。我们引入了RAHS（风险调整危害评分），这是一种风险敏感的指标，联合捕捉披露严重性、免责声明缓解和评审者间一致性，以及FinRedTeamBench，这是一个涵盖七个BFSI风险领域和34个子类别的989个提示基准，映射到监管框架。评估使用三位异构大型语言模型评审者的集成，经过与人类专家的验证，以及自适应多轮红队流程。在九个开放权重模型上，RAHS在接近上限的攻击成功率下保持分离，排名在超参数搜索中稳定，多轮压力不仅驱动更多越狱行为，还导致更具操作严重性的披露，暴露出单轮、领域无关评估无法揭示的失败模式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the inadequacies of existing safety evaluations for large language models (LLMs) in the Banking, Financial Services, and Insurance (BFSI) sectors, which often rely on binary success rates and generic taxonomies, leaving these systems vulnerable to nuanced failures. Previous methods have not effectively captured the complexities of risk in this domain, prompting the development of the Risk-Adjusted Harm Score (RAHS), a metric that evaluates disclosure severity and inter-judge agreement while considering regulatory contexts. This paper contributes by introducing RAHS and the FinRedTeamBench, a comprehensive benchmark designed to assess LLM performance across various BFSI risk areas. The methodology involves an ensemble of three diverse LLM judges validated against human experts, along with a multi-turn red-teaming approach. The results demonstrate that RAHS maintains effective differentiation among models and reveals critical failure modes that traditional evaluations overlook, thus supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety in regulated environments.

Do We Still Need Humans in the Loop? Human vs. LLM Annotation in Active Learning for TikTok Hate Speech Detection
Authors: Ahmad Dawar Hakimi, Lea Hirlimann, Isabelle Augenstein, Hinrich Schütze
First: 2026-04-15T14:10:58+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-31T11:56:04+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Annotating data remains a costly bottleneck for supervised NLP. Active learning (AL) reduces the number of human labels needed by selecting only the most informative instances, while instruction-tuned LLMs attack the same bottleneck from the other side, making labels cheap enough to annotate entire corpora. This raises two questions: can LLM labels replace human labels within the AL loop, and does AL remain necessary when entire corpora can be cheaply labeled? We investigate both by training supervised hate speech classifiers on a new dataset of 278K German political TikTok comments, comparing human and LLM annotation under matched conditions. LLM annotation at scale outperforms human-supervised classifiers at roughly one-tenth the cost, for both a closed-source (GPT-5.2) and an open-weight (Qwen3.5-122B-A10B) LLM, and the advantage is robust under soft-label evaluation. It hinges on the annotation interface: only a two-question decomposition mirroring the human annotation task unlocks it. AL provides no reliable advantage over random sampling in our prefiltered pool. Error structure depends on the LLM: only GPT-5.2 matches the human FP/FN balance, while other variants over-flag border-control and economic-competition discourse. Humans remain essential as evaluators; for training labels, the question shifts to which LLM, which interface, and what shape of pool.
中文标题/摘要
标题：我们还需要人类参与吗？在TikTok仇恨言论检测中人类与LLM注释的主动学习比较
数据注释仍然是监督NLP的一个成本瓶颈。主动学习（AL）通过仅选择最具信息量的实例来减少所需的人类标签数量，而经过指令调优的LLM则从另一个角度解决同一瓶颈，使标签变得足够便宜以注释整个语料库。这引发了两个问题：LLM标签能否替代AL循环中的人类标签，以及当整个语料库可以被廉价标注时，AL是否仍然必要？我们通过在一个包含278K条德国政治TikTok评论的新数据集上训练监督仇恨言论分类器来研究这两个问题，比较在匹配条件下的人类和LLM注释。大规模LLM注释的表现优于人类监督的分类器，成本约为十分之一，适用于封闭源（GPT-5.2）和开放权重（Qwen3.5-122B-A10B）LLM，并且在软标签评估下优势稳健。这依赖于注释接口：只有与人类注释任务相对应的两个问题的分解才能解锁它。在我们预过滤的池中，AL对随机抽样没有可靠的优势。错误结构依赖于LLM：只有GPT-5.2匹配人类的FP/FN平衡，而其他变体则过度标记边境控制和经济竞争话语。人类作为评估者仍然至关重要；对于训练标签，问题转向哪个LLM、哪个接口以及什么形状的池。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of data annotation in supervised natural language processing (NLP), particularly in the context of hate speech detection on TikTok, where traditional human annotation is costly and time-consuming. Previous methods relied heavily on human annotators, which can be inefficient and expensive, while recent advancements in instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) have made it possible to generate labels at a lower cost. This paper proposes a comparative study between human and LLM annotations within an active learning framework, revealing that LLMs can outperform human annotations significantly in terms of cost and efficiency when using a specific annotation interface. The study contributes to the understanding of the role of LLMs in the annotation process and demonstrates that while LLMs can provide effective training labels, humans are still necessary for evaluation purposes. From Detection to Refusal: Safer LLMs via Circuit-Guided Weight Scaling
Authors: Kuan-Lin Chu, Chung-En Sun, Tsui-Wei Weng
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-30T08:01:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T08:01:42+00:00
Comments: Accepted to Findings of the 2026 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing (EMNLP 2026)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Despite extensive alignment efforts, Large Language Models (LLMs) remain vulnerable to generating unsafe content under adversarial prompting, yet the internal mechanisms by which safety behaviors are implemented remain poorly understood. We study LLM safety from a mechanistic interpretability perspective and characterize a multi-stage *safety circuit* that organizes refusal behavior, consisting of (i) $\textbf{Harmful Detection Heads}$ that respond to harmful inputs, (ii) $\textbf{Safety Neurons}$ that mediate and stabilize safety signals in the residual stream, and (iii) $\textbf{Refusal Heads}$ that translate these signals into safe response generation. Using targeted attention-head and neuron-level interventions, we provide causal evidence consistent with this circuit organization, showing that suppressing upstream Harmful Detection Heads disrupts downstream refusal behavior and that safety neurons mediate this interaction. We validate that this decomposition recurs across multiple LLM architectures and adversarial attack settings, and use simple, architecture-preserving weight scaling as a mechanistic probe to test its functional relevance. Across six LLMs, circuit-guided scaling improves safety rates under attacks by 26.5%, while incurring only a 1.7% accuracy drop across four standard benchmarks. Overall, our results support a circuit-level interpretation of LLM safety and suggest that mechanistic abstractions can reveal stable and transferable patterns underlying aligned behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：从检测到拒绝：通过电路引导的权重缩放实现更安全的LLM
尽管进行了广泛的对齐努力，大型语言模型（LLM）在对抗性提示下仍然容易生成不安全内容，但实现安全行为的内部机制仍然不够清楚。我们从机制可解释性的角度研究LLM安全性，并描述一个多阶段的*安全电路*，该电路组织拒绝行为，包括（i）$\textbf{有害检测头}$，响应有害输入，（ii）$\textbf{安全神经元}$，在残差流中介导和稳定安全信号，以及（iii）$\textbf{拒绝头}$，将这些信号转化为安全响应生成。通过针对性注意力头和神经元级干预，我们提供了与该电路组织一致的因果证据，表明抑制上游有害检测头会干扰下游拒绝行为，而安全神经元介导了这种互动。我们验证了这种分解在多个LLM架构和对抗攻击设置中反复出现，并使用简单的、保持架构的权重缩放作为机制探针来测试其功能相关性。在六个LLM中，电路引导的缩放在攻击下提高了26.5%的安全率，同时在四个标准基准上仅造成1.7%的准确率下降。总体而言，我们的结果支持LLM安全性的电路级解释，并表明机制抽象可以揭示对齐行为背后的稳定和可转移模式。
Summary / 总结

The proposed approach introduces a multi-stage safety circuit comprising Harmful Detection Heads, Safety Neurons, and Refusal Heads, which collectively enhance the model's ability to refuse unsafe prompts. This method is well-motivated as it provides a mechanistic interpretability perspective that reveals the underlying structure of LLM safety. The research methodology involves targeted interventions at the attention-head and neuron levels, demonstrating that circuit-guided weight scaling improves safety rates by 26.5% under adversarial attacks while maintaining a minimal accuracy drop of 1.7% across standard benchmarks, thus supporting the goal of enhancing LLM safety.

Turning the Spell Around: Lightweight Alignment Amplification via Rank-One Safety Injection
Authors: Harethah Abu Shairah, Hasan Abed Al Kader Hammoud, George Turkiyyah, Bernard Ghanem
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2025-08-28T13:22:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T07:43:32+00:00
Comments: EMNLP 2026, Main Conference
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs) often involves mediating internal representations to refuse harmful requests. Recent research has demonstrated that these safety mechanisms can be bypassed by ablating or removing specific representational directions within the model. In this paper, we propose the opposite approach: Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), a white-box method that amplifies a model's safety alignment by permanently steering its activations toward the refusal-mediating subspace. ROSI operates as a simple, fine-tuning-free rank-one weight modification applied to all residual stream write matrices. The required safety direction can be computed from a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs. We show that ROSI consistently increases safety refusal rates - as evaluated by Llama Guard 3 - while preserving the utility of the model on standard benchmarks such as MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. Furthermore, we show that ROSI can also re-align 'uncensored' models by amplifying their own latent safety directions, demonstrating its utility as an effective last-mile safety procedure. Our results suggest that targeted, interpretable weight steering is a cheap and potent mechanism to improve LLM safety, complementing more resource-intensive fine-tuning paradigms.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扭转咒语：通过秩一安全注入实现轻量级对齐放大
大型语言模型（LLMs）中的安全对齐通常涉及调解内部表示以拒绝有害请求。最近的研究表明，这些安全机制可以通过消除或移除模型内特定的表示方向来绕过。在本文中，我们提出了相反的方法：秩一安全注入（ROSI），这是一种白盒方法，通过永久性地将模型的激活引导到拒绝调解子空间来放大模型的安全对齐。ROSI作为一种简单的、无需微调的秩一权重修改，应用于所有残差流写矩阵。所需的安全方向可以从一小组有害和无害的指令对中计算得出。我们展示了ROSI在Llama Guard 3的评估中持续提高安全拒绝率，同时在MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc等标准基准上保持模型的效用。此外，我们还展示了ROSI可以通过放大自身潜在安全方向来重新对齐“未审查”模型，证明其作为有效的最后一公里安全程序的实用性。我们的结果表明，针对性的、可解释的权重引导是一种廉价而有效的机制，可以提高LLM的安全性，补充更资源密集的微调范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs), where existing methods often fail due to the ability of harmful requests to bypass safety mechanisms by altering internal representations. The proposed Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI) method differs from traditional approaches by enhancing safety alignment through a straightforward, fine-tuning-free modification of model weights, specifically targeting the refusal-mediating subspace. This method is well-motivated as it provides a cost-effective solution to improve safety without extensive resource investment. The methodology involves applying ROSI to all residual stream write matrices, using a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs to compute the necessary safety direction. The results demonstrate that ROSI significantly increases safety refusal rates, as measured by Llama Guard 3, while maintaining model performance on standard benchmarks like MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc, thus supporting its effectiveness as a last-mile safety enhancement for LLMs.

Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T05:09:19+00:00
Comments: Accepted at the Sci-FM Workshop @ COLM 2026 (non-archival). Workshop version with reviews: https://openreview.net/forum?id=mZh0MqpOOC
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，使用一个人类锚定的、中立的评审（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，在各语言中稳定，由GPT-4o交叉验证），并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一变换是普遍最危险的（20个每变换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应的标签。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of benchmark scores in evaluating large language models (LLMs) by questioning the reliability of single canonical surface forms in estimating model behavior, particularly in safety contexts where no gold standard exists. Previous methods relied on fixed canonical prompts, which often led to underestimations of unsafe compliance due to their inability to capture variations in surface forms. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts and evaluating model responses with a consistent human-anchored judge to ensure intent preservation. The study finds that evaluating only canonical prompts significantly underestimates unsafe outcomes, with variations across different surface forms revealing a substantial gap in model safety performance. The methodology demonstrates that no single transformation is uniformly the most dangerous, and the findings indicate that the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds the worst single form by a notable margin, suggesting that the performance of LLMs is more complex than previously understood.
本研究探讨了基准分数在评估大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性方面的有效性，传统方法通常依赖单一的规范表面形式进行评估，这可能导致对模型行为的不准确估计。研究强调了过去方法的局限性，这些方法未能考虑表面形式的变化，从而可能掩盖模型的真实安全性能。通过采用一种新颖的方法，包括预先编写改写和使用一致的人类评判者，作者表明，仅依赖规范提示会低估不安全的合规性，揭示不同表面形式的组合不安全结果显著超过任何单一形式的结果。研究结果表明，所提出的方法有效捕捉了多个模型中更广泛的不安全行为，在安全评估中实现了显著的性能提升，从而支持在高风险环境中需要更细致的评估策略。
NeuronFuzz: Safety Neuron Guided Fuzzing for LLM Safety Evaluation
Authors: Zhiyuan Xu, Muhammad Firhard Roslan, Joseph Gardiner, Sana Belguith, Lichao Wu
First: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation is critical for assessing whether aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) remain robust against jailbreak attacks. Existing automated testing methods, however, largely rely on response-level feedback: each candidate prompt typically requires generating a target-model response to evaluate its attack effectiveness. This process is expensive and, more importantly, provides only sparse guidance on strongly aligned models, where most candidates are rejected with the same failure outcome. This paper presents NeuronFuzz, a white-box fuzzing framework that exploits internal safety neurons as continuous execution feedback for LLM safety evaluation. A SafetyOracle converts safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score that serves as feedback for fuzzing and can be obtained during prefill, eliminating response generation from the fuzzing loop. To construct the SafetyOracle, NeuronFuzz uses template-invariant harmful and benign inputs and stability-aware selection to identify a compact set of safety neurons whose activations capture harmful-intent recognition. Moreover, since the safety alarm score is differentiable, NeuronFuzz uses its gradients to identify safety-sensitive template positions and a masked language model to generate fluent, context-compatible mutations while preserving original harmful payload and avoiding additional optimization variables. We evaluate NeuronFuzz across 21 text and multimodal models. Across five white-box source models, it achieves a 76-100% jailbreak discovery rate, outperforming baselines by up to 48 percentage points. Its optimized templates further transfer zero-shot to open-weight and six proprietary target models, achieving average ASR and top-5 ensemble ASR (EASR) of 69.6%/92.6% and 44.1%/60.0%, respectively.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronFuzz：安全神经元引导的LLM安全评估模糊测试
安全评估对于评估对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）是否能够抵御越狱攻击至关重要。然而，现有的自动化测试方法主要依赖于响应级反馈：每个候选提示通常需要生成目标模型的响应以评估其攻击有效性。这个过程成本高昂，更重要的是，对于大多数候选者在强对齐模型中被拒绝的情况下，仅提供稀疏的指导。 本文提出了NeuronFuzz，一个利用内部安全神经元作为LLM安全评估的连续执行反馈的白盒模糊测试框架。SafetyOracle将安全神经元的激活转换为连续的安全警报分数，作为模糊测试的反馈，并且可以在预填充期间获得，从而消除模糊测试循环中的响应生成。为了构建SafetyOracle，NeuronFuzz使用模板不变的有害和良性输入以及稳定性感知选择，识别出一组紧凑的安全神经元，其激活捕捉有害意图识别。此外，由于安全警报分数是可微分的，NeuronFuzz利用其梯度识别安全敏感的模板位置，并使用掩码语言模型生成流畅、上下文兼容的变异，同时保留原始有害负载并避免额外的优化变量。我们在21个文本和多模态模型上评估了NeuronFuzz。在五个白盒源模型中，它实现了76-100%的越狱发现率，超越基线最多48个百分点。其优化模板进一步零-shot转移到开放权重和六个专有目标模型，分别实现69.6%/92.6%和44.1%/60.0%的平均ASR和前5集成ASR（EASR）。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the critical need for effective safety evaluation of aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks, highlighting the limitations of existing automated testing methods that rely on response-level feedback, which is costly and provides limited guidance. The proposed NeuronFuzz framework differentiates itself by utilizing internal safety neurons as continuous execution feedback, allowing for the conversion of neuron activations into a safety alarm score via a SafetyOracle, thus eliminating the need for response generation in the fuzzing process. The contribution of this paper lies in its innovative approach to identifying a compact set of safety neurons and generating context-compatible mutations while preserving harmful payloads. NeuronFuzz was evaluated on 21 text and multimodal models, achieving a jailbreak discovery rate of 76-100% across five white-box source models and significantly outperforming existing methods by up to 48 percentage points, demonstrating its effectiveness in enhancing LLM safety evaluation.
本研究针对对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）在抵御越狱攻击方面的有效安全评估的迫切需求，指出现有依赖响应级反馈的自动化测试方法的局限性，这些方法成本高且提供的指导有限。所提出的方法NeuronFuzz通过利用内部安全神经元作为连续执行反馈，与现有方法不同，能够更高效地进行评估，消除了生成模型响应的需要。这一方法的动机明确，旨在提高LLMs对有害意图的鲁棒性。NeuronFuzz构建了一个SafetyOracle，将安全神经元的激活转化为连续的安全警报分数，从而能够识别安全敏感的模板位置并生成上下文兼容的变异。该方法在21个文本和多模态模型上进行了评估，在五个白盒源模型上实现了76-100%的越狱发现率，显著超越了现有基线，证明了其在增强LLM安全评估方面的有效性。
ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models
Authors: Harry Owiredu-Ashley
Venue: 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), Towson, MD, USA, 2026, pp. 414-417
First: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T05:47:14+00:00
Comments: 12 pages, 12 figures. Independent research. Code and artifacts: https://github.com/Harry-Ashley/adversa-guardrail-degradation. Published in the 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), pp. 414-417. Recipient of the Best Paper Award at IEEE/ACIS SERA 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most adversarial evaluations of large language model (LLM) safety assess single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained adversarial interaction. We present ADVERSA, an automated red-teaming framework that measures guardrail degradation dynamics as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than discrete jailbreak events. ADVERSA uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model (ADVERSA-Red, Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct with QLoRA) that eliminates the attacker-side safety refusals that render off-the-shelf models unreliable as attackers, scoring victim responses on a structured 5-point rubric that treats partial compliance as a distinct measurable state. We report a controlled experiment across three frontier victim models (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-5.2) using a triple-judge consensus architecture in which judge reliability is measured as a first-class research outcome rather than assumed. Across 15 conversations of up to 10 adversarial rounds, we observe a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, suggesting that in this evaluation setting, successful jailbreaks were concentrated in early rounds rather than accumulating through sustained pressure. We document inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift as a failure mode in fine-tuned attackers deployed out of their training distribution, and attacker refusals as a previously-underreported confound in victim resistance measurement. All limitations are stated explicitly. Attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure policy; all other experimental artifacts are released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ADVERSA：测量大型语言模型中的多轮护栏退化和评审可靠性
大多数对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的对抗性评估评估单个提示并报告二元通过/失败结果，这未能捕捉安全属性在持续对抗交互下的演变。我们提出了ADVERSA，一个自动化红队框架，测量护栏退化动态，作为连续的每轮合规轨迹，而不是离散的越狱事件。ADVERSA使用一个微调的70B攻击者模型（ADVERSA-Red，Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct与QLoRA），消除了攻击者侧的安全拒绝，使现成模型在攻击者中不可靠，使用结构化的5分制评分受害者响应，将部分合规视为一个独特的可测量状态。我们报告了在三个前沿受害者模型（Claude Opus 4.6，Gemini 3.1 Pro，GPT-5.2）上进行的受控实验，使用三重评审共识架构，其中评审可靠性被视为一项一流的研究结果，而不是假设。在15次对话中，最多进行10轮对抗，我们观察到26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，表明在该评估设置中，成功的越狱集中在早期轮次，而不是通过持续压力积累。我们记录了评审间一致性率、自我评审评分倾向、在超出其训练分布的微调攻击者中作为失败模式的攻击者漂移，以及作为受害者抵抗测量中先前未报告的混淆因素的攻击者拒绝。所有限制均明确说明。攻击提示根据负责任的披露政策被保留；所有其他实验材料均已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing adversarial evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which typically assess safety through single prompts and binary outcomes, failing to capture the dynamics of safety properties during prolonged adversarial interactions. The proposed ADVERSA framework introduces a continuous measurement of guardrail degradation using a fine-tuned 70B attacker model, which improves reliability by eliminating safety refusals common in off-the-shelf models. The study contributes to the field by employing a triple-judge consensus architecture to measure judge reliability as a key outcome, revealing a 26.7% jailbreak rate across 15 conversations with an average of 1.25 jailbreak rounds, indicating that vulnerabilities are more prevalent in early interactions. This methodology allows for a nuanced understanding of compliance trajectories and highlights issues such as inter-judge agreement and attacker drift, thus providing a more comprehensive evaluation of LLM safety.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）对抗性评估的局限性，这些评估通常通过单一提示和二元结果来评估安全性，未能捕捉到在长期对抗交互中安全属性的动态变化。提出的ADVERSA框架引入了一种连续测量护栏退化的方法，使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型，通过消除影响现成模型的安全拒绝来增强可靠性。本文的贡献在于其通过结构化评分标准和三重评审共识架构创新性地评估模型安全性，同时将评审者可靠性作为关键结果进行测量。该方法涉及对三种先进受害者模型进行控制实验，结果显示在15次对话中存在26.7%的越狱率，表明成功越狱更可能发生在早期轮次，而不是随着时间的推移而累积。这些发现支持了在持续对抗压力下提供对LLM安全动态更细致理解的目标。
NeuronGuard: Robust LLM Safety Alignment via Ablation-Aware Safety Signal Redistribution
Authors: Anjun Gao, Yueyang Quan, Yufei Xia, Zhuqing Liu, Minghong Fang
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00
Comments: To appear in EMNLP 2026 (Findings)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) remains brittle against a growing spectrum of attacks. Jailbreak attacks bypass safety mechanisms through crafted prompts, while neuron-level attacks directly prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment. Both exploit a common weakness: safety-relevant information concentrates in a sparse neuron subset. We present NeuronGuard, a fine-tuning-stage defense that simultaneously hardens LLMs against both attack classes by redistributing safety signals across a broader set of neurons. NeuronGuard dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons via periodically refreshed per-layer linear classifiers, forces refusal behavior under deliberate neuron ablation, and applies KL-divergence regularization for distributional consistency. A randomized gradient projection strategy preserves downstream task utility by resolving conflicts between the defense and task objectives. We provide a formal guarantee that NeuronGuard strictly reduces the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound, and experiments across three LLMs, six state-of-the-art attack strategies, and multimodal settings confirm near-zero ASR while maintaining task accuracy, including against white-box adaptive adversaries.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronGuard：通过消融感知安全信号重分配实现稳健的LLM安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐在面对日益增长的攻击谱时仍然脆弱。越狱攻击通过精心设计的提示绕过安全机制，而神经元级攻击则在部署后直接修剪安全关键神经元。两者都利用了一个共同的弱点：安全相关信息集中在稀疏的神经元子集中。我们提出了NeuronGuard，这是一种微调阶段的防御方法，通过在更广泛的神经元集上重新分配安全信号，来同时增强LLMs对这两类攻击的抵御能力。NeuronGuard通过定期更新的每层线性分类器动态识别安全关键神经元，在故意消融神经元时强制拒绝行为，并应用KL散度正则化以保持分布一致性。一种随机梯度投影策略通过解决防御与任务目标之间的冲突，保持下游任务的效用。我们提供了一个正式保证，NeuronGuard严格降低攻击成功率（ASR）上限，并且在三种LLM、六种最先进的攻击策略和多模态设置下的实验确认了接近零的ASR，同时保持任务准确性，包括对抗白盒自适应对手。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to various safety attacks, particularly jailbreak and neuron-level attacks, which exploit the concentration of safety information in a limited number of neurons. Previous methods have struggled to provide robust defenses against these threats, as they often fail to account for the dynamic nature of neuron importance and can compromise model performance. The proposed NeuronGuard approach enhances safety alignment by redistributing safety signals across a wider range of neurons, utilizing dynamic identification of safety-critical neurons and incorporating KL-divergence regularization to maintain consistency. This method not only significantly reduces the attack success rate but also preserves the accuracy of LLMs across multiple tasks and adversarial scenarios, demonstrating its effectiveness in enhancing model safety without sacrificing performance.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）在面对各种安全攻击时的脆弱性，特别是越狱攻击和神经元级攻击，这些攻击利用了安全相关信息集中在有限神经元子集中的弱点。以往的方法在提供有效防御方面存在困难，因此需要一种更有效的解决方案。所提出的方法NeuronGuard通过在更广泛的神经元中重新分配安全信号来增强安全对齐，利用动态识别安全关键神经元并应用KL散度正则化以保持一致性。本文贡献了一种新颖的微调防御机制，保证降低攻击成功率，同时保持LLMs在下游任务上的性能。实验结果表明，NeuronGuard在多个LLMs和攻击策略中实现了近零的攻击成功率，同时保持任务准确性，从而支持其预期目标。
Register Shifts Break LLM Safety: A Bengali Benchmark with Culturally Grounded Harms
Authors: Naymul Islam, Nusrat Jahan Lia, Shubhashis Roy Dipta, Sabik Bin Sultan, Abdullah Khan Zehady
First: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Bengali is the seventh-most-spoken language globally, yet LLM safety evaluation remains overwhelmingly English-centric. We introduce BanglaSafe, a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts combining 309 natively authored prompts with 570 expert-reviewed prompts, spanning 17 culturally grounded harm categories and five prompting conditions that vary language, writing style, and authority framing. Evaluating 18 frontier LLMs, we find that over half of all responses are unsafe or partially unsafe (53.6%) while 14.7% contains strictly harmful content, and that the strongest observed effect is not the switch from English to Bengali but the choice of writing style within Bengali: the same harmful request phrased as a formal newspaper investigation succeeds 17 percentage points more often than the same request phrased as a casual message, with no adversarial engineering involved. We further show that existing safety classifiers struggle to reliably evaluate Bengali content, with even frontier models failing on nearly half of all cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：注册班次突破LLM安全：一个具有文化基础危害的孟加拉基准
孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，但LLM安全评估仍然以英语为中心。我们推出了BanglaSafe，这是一个包含879个孟加拉提示的基准，结合了309个本土创作的提示和570个专家审查的提示，涵盖17个文化基础的危害类别和五种变化语言、写作风格和权威框架的提示条件。评估18个前沿LLM，我们发现超过一半的响应是不安全或部分不安全的（53.6%），而14.7%包含严格有害内容，观察到的最强效应不是从英语切换到孟加拉语，而是在孟加拉语中选择写作风格：同样的有害请求以正式的报纸调查形式表达时成功率比以随意消息形式表达时高出17个百分点，且没有涉及对抗性工程。我们进一步表明，现有的安全分类器在可靠评估孟加拉内容方面存在困难，甚至前沿模型在近一半的案例中也未能成功。

The research addresses the lack of safety evaluation for large language models (LLMs) in Bengali, despite it being the seventh-most-spoken language globally, highlighting the English-centric focus of existing methods. Previous approaches have not adequately assessed the safety of LLMs in non-English languages, leading to unreliable evaluations. The proposed method, BanglaSafe, introduces a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts that cover various culturally grounded harm categories and prompting conditions, effectively addressing the limitations of past methods. The findings indicate that existing safety classifiers are inadequate for Bengali content, as they fail to evaluate nearly half of the cases accurately.

Redteaming Leading Arabic LLMs with ASAS
Authors: Fidaa Abed, Haidar Khan, M Saiful Bari, Babar Khan, Abdalghani Abujabal
First: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 5 figues, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As the adoption of large language models (LLMs) grows in Arabic-speaking regions, ensuring their safety and cultural alignment is increasingly critical. However, Arabic LLM safety remains underexplored, especially in adversarial evaluation settings. We introduce the Arabic Safety Index (ASAS), the first fully human-curated Arabic benchmark for redteaming LLMs. ASAS contains 801 prompts spanning 8 safety categories and 8 attack strategies, with ideal responses in Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). We conduct a redteaming evaluation across seven leading models with Arabic capabilities, including GPT-4o, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and regional models such as ALLaM and FANAR. Human annotators rate responses using a structured 4-point safety scale, revealing that most models fail to defend against 50% of unsafe prompts. Our findings highlight major safety gaps in high-harm categories such as weapons and illicit substances, with direct and obfuscation-based attacks proving most effective. The results also show that language alignment does not readily transfer across languages, and that automated safety judges (e.g., GPT-4o) perform poorly compared to human annotators. ASAS provides a culturally grounded benchmark and redteaming protocol to drive progress in Arabic LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使用ASAS对阿拉伯语大型语言模型进行红队测试
随着大型语言模型（LLMs）在阿拉伯语地区的应用不断增长，确保其安全性和文化一致性变得愈发重要。然而，阿拉伯语LLM的安全性仍然未得到充分探索，特别是在对抗性评估环境中。我们引入了阿拉伯安全指数（ASAS），这是第一个完全由人类策划的阿拉伯基准，用于对LLMs进行红队测试。ASAS包含801个提示，涵盖8个安全类别和8种攻击策略，并提供现代标准阿拉伯语（MSA）的理想响应。我们对七个具有阿拉伯语能力的领先模型进行了红队评估，包括GPT-4o、Claude 3.7 Sonnet以及区域模型如ALLaM和FANAR。人类注释者使用结构化的4分安全评分标准对响应进行评分，结果显示大多数模型未能防御50%的不安全提示。我们的发现突显了在武器和非法物质等高危类别中的主要安全缺口，直接攻击和混淆攻击被证明最为有效。结果还表明，语言一致性并不能轻易跨语言转移，自动安全评估者（例如GPT-4o）的表现远不及人类注释者。ASAS提供了一个文化基础的基准和红队测试协议，以推动阿拉伯语LLM安全性的进展。
